I just haven't felt like doing much lately. I haven't really wanted to go to my dance class... or look at the little galleries. I didn't even cook the Sunday food for my family. Usually, those are the things that make me feel like myself, but... I just didn't keep track like that.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned setting up extra calls and meetings for the green-energy proposals during that energetic stretch—thinking about your calendar, how many consecutive days did you have those extra calls scheduled before things slowed back down?\n\nPatient: I... I just haven't had the interest, I guess.\n\nDoctor: During those few days when you felt more energetic and sure of yourself, did you notice yourself talking faster than usual, feeling unusually confident, or doing things impulsively that weren't like you?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know about talking fast or doing anything crazy. But... I guess I did feel a bit different about myself. I manage properties, you know, and... well, I just started feeling like I could fix every building problem in Denver. Like, if people would just let me run with my ideas, I could do it all. \n\nWhich... I suppose that's not really how I usually talk about myself. But that was just for a little bit.\n\nDoctor: Thinking back on that energetic, confident stretch—can you tell me more precisely how many days it lasted, for example was it 3 days, 4 days, or more?\n\nPatient: I... I'm not really sure. I didn't keep track of it like that. It was just a short time... I couldn't tell you the exact number of days.\n\nDoctor: During that few days when you felt unusually energetic and confident, did anyone close to you—like your husband or family—comment that you seemed different from your usual self?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't think so. Nobody said anything to me about it. We mostly just... go about our days. So, no.\n\nDoctor: During those few days when you felt more energetic and confident, did you find yourself taking on a lot more projects or activities than usual, or doing things that were riskier than you'd normally do?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say anything risky. I just... I guess I did take on more work. \n\nI started doing these extra green-energy proposals for a few of the communities... all at the same time. I just kept setting up calls and meetings, even though my calendar was already pretty full. But... it was just work, really. That's all.\n\nDoctor: During these past two weeks of feeling low and uninterested, have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight—eating much more or less than usual?\n\nPatient: I guess my eating has been a bit off. I just... I don't really have the appetite for meals right now. Mostly I just have coffee and maybe a few bites of something during the day, and that's enough. Then at night, I might pick at some tortillas or... or maybe some sweets, but I don't really enjoy it. I just eat a little because I know I should.\n\nDoctor: During those few days when you felt more energetic and confident, did you notice your thoughts racing or jumping quickly from one idea to another?\n\nPatient: I suppose they were. I just... I had a lot on my mind. It felt like my head was bouncing from the solar panel proposals to the community budgets, and then to... to my husband being home. It was just... moving so fast, I couldn't really hold onto one thought long enough to finish it. But it was just a few days.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back to when this energetic, confident feeling started and ended in relation to something specific, like a certain weekend or workday—was it more like 3-4 days, or could it have been shorter, like just 1-2 days?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know. I didn't pay that much attention to the exact days. It was just... a few days of being busy with the proposals. I couldn't tell you if it was two days or four. I just know it passed.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned this energetic, confident stretch started around when your husband retired—can you tell me on what day of the week he retired, and then think about when you started feeling low and uninterested again? Counting the days between those two points, roughly how many days would that be?\n\nPatient: I... I don't remember the exact day he retired. It was a while ago now. I just know there were a few days where I was getting a lot done, and then... then it stopped. I couldn't tell you how many days it was between the two. I just didn't keep track like that.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned setting up extra calls and meetings for the green-energy proposals during that energetic stretch—thinking about your calendar, how many consecutive days did you have those extra calls scheduled before things slowed back down?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really look at my calendar that way. I just made the calls when I thought of them. I can't give you a number of days in a row. It was just a short stretch where I was on the phone a lot... and then I wasn't.\n\nDoctor: Thinking about that energetic, confident stretch—did it continue through an entire weekend, or did it end before the weekend even arrived?\n\nPatient: I don't really remember if it was over a weekend. It might have been. I just... I don't know exactly when it started and stopped. It was just a few days.\n\nDoctor: \n\nPatient: I don't really know what else you want me to say about it. I didn't write any of it down... it's just hard to remember.\n\nDoctor: \n\nPatient: I really don't know. I just did my work and made the calls. I didn't keep a diary of how many days it was or when it stopped. That's really all I can tell you.\n\nDoctor: \n\nPatient: I just don't know. I'm not sure what else you're looking for here... I really don't have anything else to say about it.\n\nDoctor: Aside from this recent episode, have you ever had similar stretches in the past—even briefly—where you felt unusually energetic, confident, or full of ideas, followed by a drop into low mood?\n\nPatient: I don't think so.
